Kadmeion: full-stack progress audit

17 November 2026 · Approved plan versus implemented functionality, worker candidates, acceptance and hosted evidence.

Snapshot: 7d03713 integration · 12 branches · 431 paths · 468 registered requirement records

All rows visible. Use browser Find for narrative.

Assessment

Kadmeion has a substantial private application and security foundation, but it is not the complete approved product and is not ready to be declared fully accepted. The strongest implementation is private authentication/workspace, basic project and portfolio authoring, transactional storage, scoped settings and moderation. The largest product gaps are the full editor experience, complete project-cover and verified asset rendering, public identity and publishing lifecycle, complete recovery/portability, and Phase 2 data features.

This is an evidence-based snapshot, not a new test certificate. No overall completion percentage is defensible: the inventories overlap, acceptance differs from source existence, and several large features are represented by honest unavailable states. An unavailable state is good failure handling; it does not complete the promised successful workflow.

LayerFunctionality achievedRemaining constraint
Planning and design90 historical decisions, 115 interview decisions, 52 approved LIGHT designs, 53 workflows, 138 UAT cases and 20 technical proofs are registered.Registers retain historical planning statuses; current acceptance and source mappings need consolidation. Approved mockups are not accepted software.
FrontendReact application with public/auth/workspace/editor/release/settings/growth/operator surfaces and real private data paths.Many screens expose only a subset, simulation or disabled capability. Full visual, interaction and manual accessibility acceptance remains open.
BackendSupabase Auth, owner RPCs/RLS, private Storage, transactions, release/erasure/moderation infrastructure and extensive isolated tests.Missing hosted migration; asset/cover contract acceptance; provider and live delivery acceptance. Backend completion is not established by test count.
IntegrationCurrent integration and worker branches are identifiable; verified security changes are integrated and both workflows are green.Current integration 7d03713 passes both workflows after security integration. Newer feature candidates are not automatically integrated or accepted.
Hosted operationsSupabase schema and Edge deployments exist; Vercel previews report READY.Preview readiness does not prove anonymous published portfolios, revocation, mail, independent availability or production readiness.
Phase 2Private links and manual goals/progress implemented in part.External sync, numeric DevScore, consented aggregate analytics and complete goal lifecycle remain.
Phase 3Correctly kept out of implementation.Optional AI and advanced admin analytics remain separately gated/deferred.

Audit scope and limits

Snapshot date: 17 November 2026, IST. Integration: 7d03713fbbef4f02215d38b28932ad3952adefad. All 12 fetched remote branch tips were inventoried. Shallow history was completed before comparing ancestry. There are 431 distinct tracked paths, 608 unique blob versions, 605 text versions and 3 binary versions across these tips; 11,785,941 bytes were read by the inventory scanner. Integration has 430 files, including 165 under project-docs and 264 counted application/config/test files after excluding README and project-docs.

Every text version was read and indexed programmatically for structure, requirements and status signals. Focused manual source tracing covered the principal frontend journeys, worker changes, contracts, publication/recovery boundaries, migrations, CI and governing requirements. This is not a claim of line-by-line semantic review of all 608 versions, execution of every test on every branch, or visual inspection of every approved PNG. Binary references were inventoried, not newly visually accepted. The file ledger explicitly states this distinction.

Notion sources include Session 0 and all six worker logs, the original D01–D90/UI interview record, and the LIGHT approval register. Available ChatGPT planning/interview excerpts were retrieved and cross-checked with durable records. Full raw history of every past chat, deleted chat, uncommitted worker filesystem and unpushed branch is not available from these sources; completeness is bounded by that access. Worker logs can lag newer commits; this report prefers pinned source and exact CI when they disagree.

No product code, remote branch, Notion coordination record, production configuration or data was changed for this audit. Hosted queries read schema metadata only. No new browser/SQL application suite was executed; test outcomes below come from inspected CI and explicitly dated evidence.

Approved baseline and superseded requests

  • Identity: Kadmeion, Build · Showcase · Evolve; greenfield Supabase product. DevFolio is reference-only, not an invitation to restore old architecture.
  • Architecture: React browser UI and pure editor domain code; Supabase Auth/Postgres/RLS/private Storage/RPC/Edge. No separate Express/Mongo business backend.
  • Budget: strict ₹0 recurring, not a ₹1000 fallback. Free APIs and quotas require evidence. Existing paid-tier or billing assumptions cannot be inferred from source.
  • Authoring: authenticated owner; five initial extensible presets plus genuine Blank and eight layouts; supported desktop/qualified tablet, phone preview only. Older “up to ten templates” is not the current initial-preset requirement.
  • Code: content/slot editing of supported managed code plus protected opaque owned source. Arbitrary visual reverse engineering was withdrawn. Unsafe public custom JavaScript remains blocked unless isolation is proved; static/source export fallback is approved.
  • Privacy: private profile/projects/drafts; explicit per-surface publication; no mandatory GitHub or public repository; positive-only verification; no fabricated score, users, analytics or publication.
  • Auth: email/password plus optional Google. Current owner exemption permits immediate password signup; do not silently restore mandatory email confirmation.
  • Brand: current source records Aether Drift C, original K/wordmark and later approval of the specified 12-page Canva identity set. Earlier rejected concepts remain rejected unless specifically superseded. Font/asset rights and actual visual parity still need evidence.
  • Visual acceptance: all 52 LIGHT screen images were approved; corrected full-screen preview reference remains authoritative. Later 16 November instruction permits responsive adaptation of spacing/type/density and natural scrolling. Pixel-identical fixed-frame replication is not the current acceptance rule.
  • Dark: do not generate a dark mockup set. This does not remove the approved application light/dark capability.
  • Landing: Hero → Social proof → Problem → Solution/value → How it works → FAQ → CTA. No unsupported adoption/endorsement claims.
  • Lifecycle: 15-day item Trash; account erasure separately confirmed with honest retention limits. Restricted admin may act on documented abuse, never impersonate/edit author content.
  • Phases: P1 core/auth/editor/publishing/settings/restricted operations; P2 permitted public-handle facts, private growth/DevScore and opt-in analytics; P3 optional AI/advanced admin. F0–F6 implementation checkpoints are not equivalent to completion of these product phases.

Full-stack implementation and hosted state

Declared repository stack: React 19.3.1, Vite 8.3.8, TypeScript 7.0.2, Node ≥26.13.1 <27, npm 12.2.2, Playwright 1.65.1 and axe-core 4.14.1. These are package declarations, not a claim that they are the latest public releases. Separate frontend and root checks matter because root workspaces contain contracts/editor/storage packages rather than the frontend package.

Data model: profiles/sharing, projects/exposure/assets, portfolios/revisions, onboarding, Trash, frozen release requests/pointers/events, reviewer grants, immutable artifacts/media, account exit inventory/challenges/requests, moderation cases/events, notifications/preferences, goals/milestones/progress, platform links and versioned My Components. Owner writes use RPC/constraints/RLS, not frontend visibility alone. Additional sensitive tables live in kadmeion_private.

Fresh hosted metadata: 27 public tables plus 14 private-schema tables = 41; all have RLS enabled. 65 public plus 21 private-schema SECURITY DEFINER functions = 86; none is anon-executable. No anonymous public-table SELECT grants and no public Storage buckets were returned. These are configuration facts, not a comprehensive proof that every policy/function is correct.

Migrations: hosted versions align through 20261115154500; 20261116134114_operator_appeal_evidence.sql is missing and its function is absent. No migration was applied by this audit. Older 40-table/85-function/56-migration summaries are historical checkpoints, not current counts. Do not confuse a public-schema-only count with total application tables.

New backend candidate: S1 private-assets 39acc64 contains owner-private thumbnail attachment/readback with CAS/idempotency, image/alt/ownership validation and authored HTTPS profile-link validation. The earlier SQLSTATE 42601 migration syntax failure was fixed in source at a2e0382; current candidate CI was still running at the audit cutoff. Its additional migration raises the candidate source count to 59 migrations and 265 counted files. These changes are not integrated or hosted, and the extra file-budget justification remains a coordination item.

Edge: publish, complete-private-asset and purge-expired-trash are ACTIVE version 2. Publish/asset completion have JWT verification enabled. Purge has verify_jwt=false and depends on its separate secret/feature gate; ACTIVE alone is not a security defect nor a verified safe run. No secrets were fetched.

Vercel: the Kadmeion project exists; inspected recent worker/integration deployments report READY with preview target (target=null). Their metadata ties them to source SHAs. No anonymous published portfolio, current production configuration, public access policy or end-to-end provider cost acceptance was verified by this listing.

Public security: handlers validate routes, use no-store/robots/CSP policies, bounded RPC reads, frozen byte hashes and private media rules. S4 now implements method-aware GET/HEAD verification on its worker branch; existing six GET-only fixtures fail at the earlier candidate and require S6 updates plus genuine negative tests. HEAD can prove status/headers/bodylessness, not the media bytes themselves; GET must verify bytes. A universal hash header cannot be assumed.

Account lifecycle: export, withdrawal, erasure and Trash are distinct. A database cascade cannot prove provider backups/CDN/third-party copies erased; signing out sessions does not necessarily invalidate every already-issued JWT immediately. Keep those disclosed limitations in final UX and capstone evidence.

Verification status

The refreshed integration 7d03713 passes both workflows, including authenticated React and all subsequent local HTTP/Edge/email/SQL race smoke stages. This supersedes the earlier 3c7f820 failure (14 pass/2 fail) for the integrated baseline, not newer worker features. Security candidate b3cc458 was separately green in both workflows; S6 records 511 SQL assertions and 16 authenticated browser passes there, with specific A→B→A/single-flight/discard contract tests. Mocked adapter negatives do not establish the live same-port shell's entire owner-switch matrix.

Historical F06 maps 36 of 138 UAT IDs: 12 PASS_ISOLATED and 24 PARTIAL; 102 had no exact F06 entry. It links some evidence to 26 of 53 workflows and narrow frontend samples to 14 of 20 technical proofs. Later tests extend evidence, but there is no reconciled current total proving whole-product acceptance. Do not report 36/138 as completion, or 16 browser tests as 16 whole features.

F06 automated accessibility covered 52 screen identifiers and 121 samples with zero reported violations at its checkpoint, while most screen review remained open. This does not prove WCAG 2.2 AA, visual approval, every state, independent public delivery or native-device usability. Current frontend CI includes public cases and skips authenticated cases handled in the other workflow.

Required remaining matrices: owner A/B/anon/operator; A→B→A delayed response and normal refresh; wrong/expired/withdrawn reauth; editor all buffer types and exact restoration; independent overview/case-study revisions; assets/exports with quotas and corrupt bytes; GET/HEAD canonical and direct media; all public exposure revocations; keyboard/SR/200% zoom/reduced-motion; real supported tablets; source-license and ₹0 deployment evidence.

Completion sequence and concrete deliverables

Recommended default: complete a real owner-to-visitor vertical slice while independent private features continue. Do not wait for hosted proof to build private comparison/history controls, and do not activate public features merely to make a screen appear complete.

StepLeadDeliverable and exit criterion
1. Stabilize integrationS0 + S1/S5/S6Security baseline is integrated and green. Preserve it; retain sanitized diagnostics and accept each newer feature on the combined tree. An old candidate pass must not cover newer changes.
2. Complete private media contractS1 → S2/S3Typed owner-private upload/attestation/read/attach/replace with CAS and bounded scoped pagination. S2 displays saved cover after reload; S3 assigns compatible image slot and retains exact alt/hash.
3. Finish safe editor workflowS3 + S0/S6Recovery journal/Keep Both and exact Restore bytes; protected swap choices; layout mapping/unplaced; tiered tokens; complete reusable components. Each mutation is one acknowledged transaction.
4. Finish identity/projectsS2 + S1Avatar/links, profile selection/publish contract, richer project metadata/filtering, confirmed public/link changes and authoritative interleaved CAS. Test all pending/error/retry paths.
5. Complete releases and portabilityS4 + S1/S5Private frozen comparison/history now; method-aware verifier and approved host proof next; usable publish/republish/rollback/unpublish/share. Distinct complete source/static/account archive products and roundtrip tests.
6. Finish account and operationsS5 + S1/S0Newest exports accepted; actionable inbox/preferences; session/linked-method capabilities truthful; hosted appeal RPC reconciled; restricted moderation and Trash/erasure lifecycle proved.
7. Accept P1S6 + ownerExact requirement-to-SHA evidence, hosted email/OAuth/reviewer/public delivery, all critical negative tests, manual a11y/device review and owner visual sign-off. No broad DONE based on route presence.
8. Complete P2S5/S1 + S6Finish manual goal lifecycle, then provider-specific permitted sync/provenance, explainable private score and opt-in aggregate analytics. Approved fallbacks remain available.
9. Final capstone packageS0/document ownerReplace planning tense with actual architecture/results, genuine screens, diagrams, licenses, references, measured limitations and reproducible demo. P3 stays future work unless separately authorized.

Immediate task ownership already exists for thumbnails, recovery, assets, exports and GET/HEAD. Explicitly add bounded tasks for the less-visible omissions: profile publish/avatar/social links; handle rename; per-surface project exposure; autosave semantics; Favorites; layout mapping; scoped typography/tokens; source+asset import; live release timeline/republish; visitor report UX; permanent deletion and session directory capability; global recovery/glyph consistency; accessibility and capstone reconciliation. This is a proposed backlog from the audit, not a claim that worker assignments were changed.

Retrospective, premortem and improvements

What worked: sole-file ownership, isolated worker branches, immutable revision/CAS design, private defaults, truthful unavailable states, real isolated SQL/browser tests and exact commit evidence prevented many false completion claims. The security candidate demonstrates that coordinated backend/consumer/testing changes can pass together.

What slowed completion: old planning statuses coexist with later implementation logs; workers repeatedly checked broad suites while specific capabilities had no bounded implementation task; a shared reauth/epoch dependency generated failures on otherwise unrelated branches; frontend CI initially missed integration-target PRs; diagnostic artifacts did not retain the exact failure context; preview deployment success and route coverage could be mistaken for functional delivery. These are evidence-based process observations, not blame or a root-cause diagnosis of every failure.

Failure modeWarning signMitigation
Finish 52 screens but not the productDisabled primary action or a “not available” panel counted as doneAccept observable successful journey plus failure path against exact WF/UI IDs.
Workers collide or integrate stale dependenciesForeign-file deltas, multiple candidate branches, green checks from older SHAOne writer per file; dependency pin; S0 integrates owned delta and tests combined tree.
Data loss on reload/conflictLocal state clears before durable acknowledgement; same cloud/local revision assumedPersist buffer copies, exact-byte restoration, no automatic rebase, cross-tab tests.
Public privacy promise failsCanonical 404 but direct asset/cache remains reachableReal GET/HEAD/direct-media tests and fresh revocation receipt before success claims.
Provider dependency stalls all workWorkers wait for email/host proof while private features remain missingParallel independent private UI/contracts; isolate external enablement gate.
Evidence disappears or driftsOne-day artifacts, Notion log behind commit, historical case labels treated currentDurable sanitized evidence with tested SHA/environment/scenario and a generated current register.
Free tier or browser eviction destroys usabilityNo tested quota warning/export path; assumed indefinite local/host persistenceBounded quotas, backups/export, independent static usage, explicit retention limits.

Three high-value improvements: (1) Generate one current requirement ledger from structured evidence rather than editing several conflicting status documents; store implementation, integration, test and hosted/manual dimensions separately. (2) Give each worker one observable capability plus one repair queue, with a work-in-progress limit; move on after relevant tests pass. (3) Maintain one S0 integration candidate for the current dependency batch, while preserving original worker branches; avoid treating special test candidates as merge-ready product branches.

Academic deliverables: 15 capstone documents exist as planning material/templates. Final report, real screenshots, measured results, accurate ER/architecture diagrams, demonstration dataset, presentation/viva evidence, source licenses and institution-specific front matter remain to be completed against the delivered system. Historical six-week/10–20-hour assumptions do not establish current schedule feasibility.

Browser adapter contract declaration inventory

Base: integration supabase-auth-port.ts. The security integration includes recover/discard additions; the newer private-assets candidate adds further contracts. Adapter methods include session APIs and helpers and are not unique backend endpoints. The initial 49-method register is older.
